<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-81E9148A-2F48-4754-A14C-C49051FB1236'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Circle the number in the list below which is closest to the value $1$

$0.993   \quad 1.006  \quad 1.1  \quad 0.94  \quad 0.99$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let's calculate the difference between the number and $1$.

| Number   |     Difference      |
|:--------:|:-------------------:|
|  $0.993$ | $1 - 0.993 = 0.007$ |
|  $1.006$ | $1.006 - 1 = 0.006$ |
|  $1.1$   | $1.1 - 1 = 0.1$     |
|  $0.94$  | $1 - 0.94 = 0.06$   |
|  $0.99$  | $1 - 0.99 = 0.01$   |

From the calculation above, the smallest difference is $0.006$.

Number closest in value $= 1.006$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$1.006$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let's calculate the difference between the number and $1$.

| Number   |     Difference      |
|:--------:|:-------------------:|
|  $0.993$ | $1 - 0.993 = 0.007$ |
|  $1.006$ | $1.006 - 1 = 0.006$ |
|  $1.1$   | $1.1 - 1 = 0.1$     |
|  $0.94$  | $1 - 0.94 = 0.06$   |
|  $0.99$  | $1 - 0.99 = 0.01$   |

From the calculation above, the smallest difference is $0.006$.

Number closest in value $= 1.006$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$1.006$
</code></pre>

Give a reason for your answer.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{array}{llll}
\dfrac{3}{5} & & 70\% \\\\
\dfrac{3 \times 2}{5 \times 2} & & \dfrac{7\cancel0}{10\cancel0} \\\\
\dfrac{6}{10} & & \dfrac{7}{10} \\\\
\dfrac{6}{10} & < & \dfrac{7}{10} \\\\
\dfrac{3}{5} & < & 70\% \\\\
\end{array}
$

Therefore, $70\%$ is greater than $\dfrac{3}{5}$

</div>
<div class='working'>

**Row 1**

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
&= (7 - 3) \times 2 \\\\
&= 4 \times 2 \\\\
&= 8 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

**Row 2**

Let the input $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
(a - 3) \times 2 &= 34 \\\\
a - 3 &= \dfrac{34}{2} \\\\
a - 3 &= 17 \\\\
a &= 20 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

**Row 3**

Let the input $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
(a - 3) \times 2 &= 0 \\\\
a - 3 &= \dfrac{0}{2} \\\\
a - 3 &= 0 \\\\
a &= 3 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
(a - 3) \times 2 &= a \\\\

a - \dfrac{a}{2} &= 3 \\\\
\dfrac{a}{2} &= 3 \\\\
a &= 3 \times 2 \\\\
a &= 6 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

A different number machine produces the input and output table below.

![missing table](/papers/missing_table.svg)


What is the rule for this number machine?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the output $= x$

Let the rule $= a \times x + b$

From Row 1, where $x = 2$,

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times 2 + b &= 6 \\\\
2a + b &= 6 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

From Row 2, where $x = 4$,

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times 4 + b &= 7 \\\\
4a + b &= 7 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

Lets solve the equations for \' a \'

$
\begin{aligned}
4a + b &= 7 \\\\
4a + (6 - 2a) &= 7 \\\\
4a - 2a &= 7 - 6 \\\\
2a &= 1 \\\\
a &= \dfrac{1}{2} \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

Lets solve the equations for \' b \'

$
\begin{aligned}
2a + b &= 6 \\\\
2 \times \dfrac{1}{2} + b &= 6 \\\\
1 + b &= 6 \\\\
b &= 5 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

Therefore, the rule $= \dfrac{x}{2} + 5$

Lets verify the same for Row 3

$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac{x}{2} + 5 \\\\
&= \dfrac{8}{2} + 5 \\\\
&= 4 + 5 \\\\
&= 9 = 9 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

Lets verify the same for Row 4

$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac{x}{2} + 5 \\\\
&= \dfrac{12}{2} + 5 \\\\
&= 6 + 5 \\\\
&= 11 = 11 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

The rule is **Divide the number by 2 and add 5**

</div>
<div class='working'>

TODOWORKING

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

Divide the number by 2 and add 5

</div>
<div class='answer'>

TODOANSWER

</div>
</div>

<div class='workingcodeblock'>

&= 800
\end{aligned}

<div class='question subquestion'>

Which number is $100$ times smaller than $349$?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin {aligned}
&= \dfrac {1} {100} \times 349 \\\\
&= \dfrac {349} {100} \\\\
&= 3.49
\end {aligned}
$

$3.49$ is $100$ times smaller than $349$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>

\end{aligned}
$

<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

$4 \times \boxed{\phantom{1}} + 8 = 44$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
4 \times a + 8  &= 44 \\\\
4 a             &= 44 - 8 \\\\
4 a             &= 36 \\\\
a               &= \dfrac {36} {4} \\\\
a               &= \dfrac {9 \times 4} {4} \\\\
a               &= \dfrac {9 \times \cancel {4}} {\cancel {4}} \\\\
a               &= 9
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$9$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
4 \times a + 8  &= 44 \\\\
4 a             &= 44 - 8 \\\\
4 a             &= 36 \\\\
a               &= \dfrac {36} {4} \\\\
a               &= \dfrac {9 \times 4} {4} \\\\
a               &= \dfrac {9 \times \cancel {4}} {\cancel {4}} \\\\
a               &= 9
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

</div>

</code></pre>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

$3 \times (5 - \boxed{\phantom{1}}) = 12$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
3 \times (5 - a)    &= 12 \\\\
15 - 3 a            &= 12 \\\\
3 a                 &= 15 - 12 \\\\
3 a                 &= 3 \\\\
a                   &= 1
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$1$

</div>
</div>

\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

$40   \div  \ \boxed{\phantom{1}} = 4^2 \div 2$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
40 \div a         &= 4 ^ 2 \div 2 \\\\
\dfrac {40} {a}   &= 4 \times 4 \div 2 \\\\
\dfrac {40} {a}   &= \dfrac {4 \times 4} {2} \\\\
a                 &= \dfrac {40 \times 2} {4 \times 4} \\\\
a                 &= \dfrac {4 \times 2 \times 5 \times 2} {4 \times 2 \times 2} \\\\
a                 &= \dfrac {\cancel {4} \times \cancel {2} \times 5 \times \cancel {2}} {\cancel {4} \times \cancel {2} \times \cancel {2}} \\\\
a                 &= 5
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$5$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number $= a$

a                 &= \dfrac {4 \times 2 \times 5 \times 2} {4 \times 2 \times 2} \\\\
a                 &= \dfrac {\cancel {4} \times \cancel {2} \times 5 \times \cancel {2}} {\cancel {4} \times \cancel {2} \times \cancel {2}} \\\\
a                 &= 5
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-AFFC377F-8DB8-4D17-8980-0952A23564BA'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

In the following diagrams, the number underneath is calculated using a rule.

The rule is **"multiply the numbers in the white regions and then add the numbers in the grey regions."**

The total is written underneath the diagram.

For example:

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


Fill in the dotted line to complete the following diagrams:

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

From first image,

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 13 \times 3 + 7 + 8 \\\\
&= 39 + 15 \\\\
&= 54 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

From second image,

Let the missing number $= a$

$
\begin{aligned}
12 \times 4 + a + 6 &= 55 \\\\
48 + a + 6 &= 55 \\\\
54 + a &= 55 \\\\
a &= 55 - 54 \\\\
a &= 1 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

From third image,

Let the first missing number $= a$

Let the second missing number $= b$

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times b + 9 + 8 &= 38 \\\\
ab + 17 &= 38 \\\\
ab &= 38 - 17 \\\\
ab &= 21 \\\\
a \times b &= 1 \times 21 \ \text{Or,}\ \\\\
a \times b &= 3 \times 7 \ \text{Or,}\ \\\\
a \times b &= 7 \times 3 \ \text{Or,}\ \\\\
a \times b &= 21 \times 1 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
<div class='working'>

TODOWORKING

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$
55 \\\\
1 \\\\
(1, 21) \ \text{Or,}\ (3, 7) \ \text{Or,}\ (7, 3) \ \text{Or,}\ (21, 1) \\\\
$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

TODOANSWER

</div>
</div>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Which number is $1000$ times bigger than $34.2$?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>



ABBR: Let's first multiply the numbers ignoring the decimal points.


$
1000 \times 342 = 342000 \\
$

Between the two numbers, the total number of digits after the decimal point is one. Let's account for the decimal point now.

$
= 34200
$

$34200$ is $1000$ times bigger than $34.2$.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$34200$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>

rtq_abbr_md_multiply_when_decimal_points_note

$
1000 \times 342 = 342000 \\
$

Between the two numbers, the total number of digits after the decimal point is one. Let's account for the decimal point now.

$
= 34200
$

$34200$ is $1000$ times bigger than $34.2$.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$34200$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Which number is *one hundred* less than *two thousand and forty?*

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin {aligned}
&= 2040 - 100 \\\\
&= 1940
\end {aligned}
$

*Nineteen hundred and forty* is *one hundred* less than *two thousand and forty*.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$1940$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin {aligned}
&= 2040 - 100 \\\\
&= 1940
\end {aligned}
$

*Nineteen hundred and forty* is *one hundred* less than *two thousand and forty*.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Sally has the number machine shown below.

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


Complete the table of input and output numbers for Sally’s machine.

![missing table](/papers/missing_table.svg)


</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

**Row 1**

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 6 \times 4 - 3 \\\\
&= 24 - 3 \\\\
&= 21 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

**Row 2**

Let input $= a$

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times 4 - 3 &= 33 \\\\
a \times 4 &= 33 + 3 \\\\
a \times 4 &= 36 \\\\
a &= \dfrac{36}{4} \\\\
a &= 9 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

**Row 3**

Let input $= a$

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times 4 - 3 &= -1 \\\\
a \times 4 &= -1 + 3 \\\\
a \times 4 &= 2 \\\\
a &= \dfrac{2}{4} \\\\
a &= \dfrac{1}{2} \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$21, 9, \dfrac{1}{2}$

</div>
</div>

<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Imogen has a different number machine which produced the following table of input and output numbers.

![missing table](/papers/missing_table.svg)


Unfortunately, the labels have fallen off Imogen’s machine.

Write suitable labels on the diagram below.

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the input $= x$

Let the rule $= a \times x + b$

From Row 1, where $x = -1$

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times -1 + b &= 0 \\\\
b - a &= 0 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

From Row 2, where $x = 0$,

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times 0 + b &= 2 \\\\
b &= 2 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

Lets solve the equations for \' a \'

$
\begin{aligned}
b - a &= 0 \\\\
a &= b \\\\
a &= 2 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

Therefore, the rule $= 2 \times x + 2$

Lets verify the same for Row 3

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 2 \times x + 2 \\\\
&= 2 \times 1 + 2 \\\\
&= 2 + 2 \\\\
&= 4 = 4 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

Lets verify the same for Row 4

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 2 \times x + 2 \\\\
&= 2 \times 2 + 2 \\\\
&= 4 + 2 \\\\
&= 6 = 6 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

a, b $= 2, 2$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\times 2 \ \text{then}\ + 2$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the input $= x$

\end{aligned}

$

a &= b \\\\
a &= 2 \\\\
\end{aligned}

Therefore, the rule $= 2 \times x + 2$

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 2 \times x + 2 \\\\
&= 2 \times 1 + 2 \\\\
&= 2 + 2 \\\\
&= 4 = 4 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

Lets verify the same for Row 4

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 2 \times x + 2 \\\\
&= 2 \times 2 + 2 \\\\
&= 4 + 2 \\\\
&= 6 = 6 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

a, b $= 2, 2$
</code></pre>

\begin{aligned}
&= 17 \times 540 \\\\
&= 17 \times (538 + 2) \\\\
&= 17 \times 538 + 17 \times 2 \\\\
&= 9146 + 34 \\\\
&= 9180
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>

$

<div class='question question'>

Given that $7.84 \times 6 = 46.8$, work out the value of $7.84 \times 18$ 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>



$
\begin{aligned}
&=7.84 \times 18 \\\\
&=7.84 \times 6 \times 3 \\\\
&=46.8 \times 3\\\\
&=140.4     
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$140.4$

</div>
</div>

<div class='question subquestion'>

The number machine below changes numbers according to the rule:

**multiply by $3$, then add $6$**

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


Complete the input and output numbers for the machine.

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let output $= a$

$
\begin{aligned}
& 40 \times 3 + 6 &= a \\\\
& 120 + 6 &= a \\\\
& a &= 126 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

Let the input $= b$

$
\begin{aligned}
& b \times 3 + 6 &= 54 \\\\
& 3b + 6 &= 54 \\\\
& 3b &= 54 - 6 \\\\
& 3b &= 48 \\\\
& b &= \dfrac{48}{3} \\\\
& b &= 16 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
<div class='working'>

TODOWORKING

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$126 \\\\ 16$

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

There are two possible function machines which will give results shown below. 

Work out both possible answers.

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let first label $= \times a$

Let second label $= + b$

$
\begin{aligned}
& a \times 6 + b &= 12 \\\\
& 6a + b &= 12 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
& a \times 8 + b &= 20 \\\\
& 8a + b &= 20 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

Lets solve for \'a\'

$
\begin{aligned}
& 8a + b &= 20 \\\\
& 8a + (12 - 6a) &= 20 \\\\
& 8a - 6a &= 20 - 12 \\\\
& 2a &= 8 \\\\
& a &= \dfrac{8}{2} \\\\
& a &= 4 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

Lets solve for \'b\'

$
\begin{aligned}
& 6a + b &= 12 \\\\
& 6(4) + b &= 12 \\\\
& 24 + b &= 12 \\\\
& b &= 12 - 24 \\\\
& b &= - 12 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

Therefore, the labels $= \times 4, -12$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Second possible labels

Let first label $= + a$

Let second label $= \times b$

$
\begin{aligned}
(6 + a) \times b &= 12 \\\\
6b + ab &= 12 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
(8 + a) \times b &= 20 \\\\
8b + ab &= 20 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

Lets solve for \'b\'

$
\begin{aligned}
8b + (12 - 6b) &= 20 \\\\
8b - 6b &= 20 - 12 \\\\
2b &= 8 \\\\
b &= \dfrac{8}{2} \\\\
b &= 4 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

Lets solve for \'a\'

$
\begin{aligned}
(6 + a) \times 4 &= 12 \\\\
6 \times 4 + a \times 4 &= 12 \\\\
24 + 4a &= 12 \\\\
4a &= 12 - 24 \\\\
4a &= -12 \\\\
a &= \dfrac{-12}{4} \\\\
a &= -3 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

Therefore, the labels $= + 4, \times -3$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\times 4, - 12$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

$+ 4, \times -3$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let first label $= \times a$

Let second label $= + b$

$
\begin{aligned}
& a \times 6 + b &= 12 \\\\
& 6a + b &= 12 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
& a \times 8 + b &= 20 \\\\
& 8a + b &= 20 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

Lets solve for \'a\'

$
\begin{aligned}
& 8a + b &= 20 \\\\
& 8a + (12 - 6a) &= 20 \\\\
& 8a - 6a &= 20 - 12 \\\\
& 2a &= 8 \\\\
& a &= \dfrac{8}{2} \\\\
& a &= 4 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

Lets solve for \'b\'

$
\begin{aligned}
& 6a + b &= 12 \\\\
& 6(4) + b &= 12 \\\\
& 24 + b &= 12 \\\\
& b &= 12 - 24 \\\\
& b &= - 12 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

Therefore, the labels $= \times 4, -12$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Second possible labels

Let first label $= + a$

Let second label $= \times b$

$
\begin{aligned}
(6 + a) \times b &= 12 \\\\
6b + ab &= 12 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
(8 + a) \times b &= 20 \\\\
8b + ab &= 20 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

Lets solve for \'b\'

$
\begin{aligned}
8b + (12 - 6b) &= 20 \\\\
8b - 6b &= 20 - 12 \\\\
2b &= 8 \\\\
b &= \dfrac{8}{2} \\\\
b &= 4 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

Lets solve for \'a\'

$
\begin{aligned}
(6 + a) \times 4 &= 12 \\\\
6 \times 4 + a \times 4 &= 12 \\\\
24 + 4a &= 12 \\\\
4a &= 12 - 24 \\\\
4a &= -12 \\\\
a &= \dfrac{-12}{4} \\\\
a &= -3 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

Therefore, the labels $= + 4, \times -3$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\times 4, - 12$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$+ 4, \times -3$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>

<div class='question subquestion'>

Which number is $100$ times smaller than $56.9$?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin {aligned}
&= \dfrac {1} {100} \times 56.9 \\\\
&= \dfrac {56.9} {100} \\\\
&= 0.569 
\end {aligned}
$

$0.569$ is $100$ times smaller than $56.9$.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$0.569$

</div>
</div>

$

</code></pre>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What is the difference between the largest and smallest $4$-digit numbers that can be made?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Largest 4-digit number that can be made $= 8765$

Smallest 4-digit number that can be made $= 1256$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Difference}\ &= \text{Largest 4-digit number}\ - \text{Smallest 4-digit number}\ \\\\
&= 8765 - 1256 \\\\
&= 7509 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$7509$

</div>
</div>

&= 8765 - 1256 \\\\
&= 7509 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-614F23B2-E8EA-4EB8-83F5-64E2D37B50E5'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Becca is thinking of a prime number bigger than $20$

When she writes its digits in reverse order, the new number is also prime.


What is the smallest number Becca could be thinking of?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$31$

Lets verify,

On revesing $31$, $13$ is also a prime.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$31$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$31$

Lets verify,

On revesing $31$, $13$ is also a prime.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$31$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-545D36CE-6DD5-47EA-A5C4-E9E98DA2C468'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

A number machine has produced the following table of input and output numbers.

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


Write suitable labels on the diagram below.

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the input $= x$

Let the rule $= a \times x + b$

From Row 1, where $x = -1$

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times -1 + b &= 1 \\\\
b - a &= 1 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

From Row 2, where $x = 0$

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times 0 + b &= 1.5 \\\\
b &= 1.5 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

Lets solve for \'a\',

$
\begin{aligned}
b - a &= 1 \\\\
1.5 - a &= 1 \\\\
a &= 0.5 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

Therefore, the rule $= 0.5 \times x + 1.5$

Lets verify,

From Row 3, where $x = 2$

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 0.5 \times 2 + 1.5 \\\\
&= 1 + 1.5 \\\\
&= 2.5 = 2.5 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

From Row 4, where $x = 5$

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 0.5 \times 5 + 1.5 \\\\
&= 2.5 + 1.5 \\\\
&= 4 = 4 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

Therefore, the labels are **$\times 0.5$** , **$+ 1.5$**

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\times 0.5, + 1.5$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the input $= x$

Let the rule $= a \times x + b$

From Row 1, where $x = -1$

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times -1 + b &= 1 \\\\
b - a &= 1 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

From Row 2, where $x = 0$

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times 0 + b &= 1.5 \\\\
b &= 1.5 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

Lets solve for \'a\',

$
\begin{aligned}
b - a &= 1 \\\\
1.5 - a &= 1 \\\\
a &= 0.5 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

Therefore, the rule $= 0.5 \times x + 1.5$

Lets verify,

From Row 3, where $x = 2$

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 0.5 \times 2 + 1.5 \\\\
&= 1 + 1.5 \\\\
&= 2.5 = 2.5 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

From Row 4, where $x = 5$

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 0.5 \times 5 + 1.5 \\\\
&= 2.5 + 1.5 \\\\
&= 4 = 4 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

Therefore, the labels are **$\times 0.5$** , **$+ 1.5$**
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-9AB690F5-D948-4D6F-96C8-8B6598F5771D'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

To find the digital root of a number, you add the digits repeatedly until you reach a single digit number.

For example, the digital root of $169$ is $7$ because $1 + 6 + 9 = 16$, and $1 + 6 = 7$

The digital roots of the first $9$ square numbers are given in the table below:

![missing table](/papers/missing_table.svg)


</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

</div>
</div>

</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Complete the table of digital roots of the next nine square numbers.
![missing table](/papers/missing_table.svg)


</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Digital root of} \ 100 &= 1 + 0 + 0 \\\\
&= 1 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Digital root of} \ 121 &= 1 + 2 + 1 \\\\
&= 4 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Digital root of} \ 144 &= 1 + 4 + 4 \\\\
&= 9
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Digital root of} \ 196 &= 1 + 9 + 6 &= 16 \\\\
&= 1 + 6 \\\\
&= 7 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Digital root of} \ 225 &= 2 + 2 + 5 \\\\
&= 9 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Digital root of} \ 256 &= 2 + 5 + 6 &= 13 \\\\
&= 1 + 3 \\\\
&= 4 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Digital root of} \ 289 &= 2 + 8 + 9 &= 19 \\\\
&= 1 + 9 &= 10 \\\\
&= 1 + 0 \\\\
&= 1
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

1, 4, 9, 7, 9, 4, 1

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Digital root of} \ 100 &= 1 + 0 + 0 \\\\
&= 1 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Digital root of} \ 121 &= 1 + 2 + 1 \\\\
&= 4 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Digital root of} \ 144 &= 1 + 4 + 4 \\\\
&= 9
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}

\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Digital root of} \ 225 &= 2 + 2 + 5 \\\\

\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Digital root of} \ 256 &= 2 + 5 + 6 &= 13 \\\\

&= 4 \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Digital root of} \ 289 &= 2 + 8 + 9 &= 19 \\\\
&= 1 + 9 &= 10 \\\\
&= 1 + 0 \\\\
&= 1
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What patterns do you notice in the digital roots in the tables above?

</div>

\begin{aligned}

&= \dfrac {700} {1000} \\\\

\end{aligned}
$

<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
17 + a  &= 23 \times 2 \\\\
17 + a  &= 46 \\\\
a       &= 46 - 17 \\\\
a       &= 29 
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$29$

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

$9 + (5 \times 7) = 47 - \boxed{\phantom{11}}$ 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
9 + (5 \times 7)  &= 47 - a \\\\
9 + 35            &= 47 - a \\\\
44                &= 47 - a\\\\
a                &= 47 - 44 \\\\
a                 &= 3 
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$3$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number $= a$

\begin{aligned}
9 + (5 \times 7)  &= 47 - a \\\\
9 + 35            &= 47 - a \\\\
44                &= 47 - a\\\\
a                &= 47 - 44 \\\\
a                 &= 3 
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>

<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

$\boxed{\phantom{11}}  \ \times 16 = 36 \div 9$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times 16  &= 36 \div 9 \\\\
a \times 16  &= \dfrac {36} {9} \\\\
a            &= \dfrac {36} {9 \times 16} \\\\
a            &= \dfrac {9 \times 4} {9 \times 4 \times 4} \\\\
a            &= \dfrac {\cancel {9} \times \cancel {4}} {\cancel {9} \times 4 \times \cancel {4}} \\\\
a            &= \dfrac {1} {4}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac {1} {4}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times 16  &= 36 \div 9 \\\\
a \times 16  &= \dfrac {36} {9} \\\\
a            &= \dfrac {36} {9 \times 16} \\\\
a            &= \dfrac {9 \times 4} {9 \times 4 \times 4} \\\\
a            &= \dfrac {\cancel {9} \times \cancel {4}} {\cancel {9} \times 4 \times \cancel {4}} \\\\
a            &= \dfrac {1} {4}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\dfrac {1} {4}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>

<div class='workings'>

$
\begin {aligned}
&= 90 \div 12 \\\\
&= \dfrac {90} {12} \\\\
&= \dfrac {90 \times 10} {12 \times 10} \\\\
&= \dfrac {900} {12 \times 10} \\\\
&= \dfrac {75} {10} \\\\
&= 7.5
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>

$7.5$

</div>

\begin {aligned}

&= \dfrac {90 \times 10} {12 \times 10} \\\\
&= \dfrac {900} {12 \times 10} \\\\
&= \dfrac {75} {10} \\\\
&= 7.5
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
